# Bug inventory

Every known problem of the retail game that ReCoF may fix, one row per issue. The sources are the user's reports, the 180 bugs DeCoF found during the decompilation ([original_bugs.md](original_bugs.md)), remarks in DeCoF's progress and session logs, the runtime patches of RebuiltSimon and of the community co-op mod, and public reports. Each row was checked against the source unless its evidence says otherwise.

## Columns

- **ID** is permanent. A new issue takes the next free number in whatever section it belongs to, and numbers are never reused. Fix commits name it: `<binary>: <what was fixed> (<id>)`.
- **Repro** says how to see the problem in game. "None" means there is no visible effect in the shipped game.
- **Severity**, from worst to least:
  - **crash**: the game crashes, hangs or corrupts memory.
  - **save**: saves lose or corrupt state, or persistent progress is wrong.
  - **gameplay**: game logic is wrong, softlocks included.
  - **performance**: frame rate, loading time or memory growth.
  - **visual**: the picture is wrong.
  - **audio**: a sound is wrong, missing or doubled.
  - **cosmetic**: text and menu details.
  - **latent**: wrong code with no visible effect in the shipped game.
- **Runners** is yes when speedrunners rely on the behavior, check when a fix could change something they use and that has to be checked first, and no otherwise. A fix to a yes or check row keeps the original behavior available behind an `rc_` cvar.
- **Status** is open, test (fixed in the source and waiting for an in-game test), fixed (confirmed in game) or keep (decided not to fix, with the reason in the fix plan). `git log --grep <id>` finds the fix commits.

Rows imported from DeCoF can hold several related problems of one function or file. A fix commit names the part it fixes, and the row is fixed when all its parts are.

| B001 | client.dll, hw.dll | See the causes in the evidence. | Frame rate gets very bad after the game has been open for a long time. | Play a long session across several maps without restarting, and compare the frame rate in the same spot early and late. | User report. Likely causes in the source: particle systems are never removed until the next map load (B189); hovering an inventory item reloads and leaks its picture every frame (B190); VGUI images are never freed (B191, B249); the 128 MB heap cap makes the cache evict and reload (B250); model and sprite textures stay for the whole session (B251); the Cg context and shaders leak on every map load (B192); irrKlang sound handles are never dropped (B173); the busy-wait frame limiter may cause thermal throttling (B252). None of it is measured yet. | performance | check | Measure first: log the frame rate, private bytes, GPU memory and texture counts at the start and after two hours, then fix the causes in the order the numbers show. The inventory hover and particle leaks are cheap first fixes. Runners: the frame limiter row changes frame timing. | open |
| B002 | hl.dll | See the causes in the evidence. | AI breaks and monsters stop moving after the game has been open for a long time. | Long session; monsters stop moving or reacting. Note fps_max, developer and the map when it happens. | User report. Candidate in the source: monster turn speed scales with the frame time, so at high or drifting frame rates monsters stand turning instead of acting (B117). Float level time was ruled out for sessions of hours (B262). Not explained yet. | gameplay | check | Add a debug command that prints the server time, the frame time and a monster's think state, reproduce it in a long session, then fix the cause. Runners: the turn speed fix goes behind an rc_ cvar. Sprint 4 candidate: every monster's eye point (view_ofs) was uninitialized stack data in retail (B085), so what a monster can see depended on leftover memory; fixed in the test build, so check whether AI still stops after a long session. | open |
| B003 | hl.dll, hw.dll | See the causes in the evidence. | AI sometimes moves through walls as if it had noclip. | Let a faster or slower chase you through a closed thin door or a thin wall. | User report. Likely cause: Cry of Fear's humanoid monsters use an 8 unit box, so the engine collides them as a point, and their step never traces sideways (B118). Also possible: scripted sequence ends that move a monster without a trace (B119), the slower's uneven hull (B031), and the engine skipping the rest of an area node after one rejected entity (B213). | gameplay | check | Test cof_monsterhumanscale 16 at the reported spots, then give small monsters a swept move behind an rc_ cvar. Runners: monster blocking can be part of routes. Sprint 4 candidate for the seeing part: the monster eye point was uninitialized (B085), fixed in the test build; the movement causes (B118, B119, B031, B213) are still open. Sprint 4: point sized monsters no longer step through thin walls and closed doors (B118) and the eye point is fixed (B085), both in the test build; B119 (scripted sequence ends) and B213 are next. | open |
| B004 | unknown | See the causes in the evidence. | c_lake crashes at random with cg_water 0, so runners turn cg_water on there. | c_lake with cg_water 0, ride the boat until it crashes; catch it with a debugger or a minidump. | User report. With cg_water 0 the engine draws the lake's func_water itself, and no faulty code was found on that path yet (B284). Related bugs on the water code paths: B205. | crash | yes | Get one crash address from a minidump and fix that site. Runners: they use cg_water 1 as the workaround, so both settings must stay stable. Reproduce once on c_lake with cg_water 0; the crash handler writes the dump to Steam's dumps folder (see Crash dumps). | open |
| B005 | hl.dll | See the causes in the evidence. | Buffer overflow in co-op only, fixed at runtime by the community co-op mod. | Co-op with two players, a joining player's model changed while the map loads. | User report; the mod is https://github.com/UNIk0z/Cry-of-fear-Co-op-mod-SteamEdition. Its diagnosis, confirmed in our source: in co-op every userinfo change forces the player model back to the slot model, so any other writer of the model starts a loop of full player updates until a loading client gets a reliable overflow (B120). Other co-op memory bugs: B121, B097, and the voice data room check (B231). | crash | no | Give the forced model a single source of truth so no loop can start; fix the other co-op memory bugs in their own rows. Sprint 3 check: the overflow the co-op mod fixed was its own (its per frame model writer fought the forced slot model, cof-coop docs/BUG_TRACKER.md line 59), so B120 is latent in vanilla; the vanilla co-op level change disconnects with reliable buffer warnings (Steam reports of 2021 and 2025, cof-coop docs/NETWORK_OVERFLOW.md) are still unexplained. Next step: a host console log with developer 1 from a co-op level change that drops a player, to see which messages fill the reliable channel. | open |
| B006 | hl.dll | See the causes in the evidence. | In the final wheelchair cutscene, pressing inputs a few milliseconds after it ends softlocks the game. | c_ending4b: kill the last book Simon; about 6 s later look down about 45 degrees and press use. | User report. Likely cause: when the fight ends, Disembark puts Simon inside the chair and re-arms its use, so a use press right after mounts it again; the ending then runs with the player frozen and the view stuck on the chair (B123). In c_ending4 the end of the memory cutscene and the level change are only about 0.2 s apart and can swap at low frame rates (B285). The chair's animation events also fire twice (B060). | gameplay | yes | Do not re-arm the chair's use after this disembark, and put the player beside the chair with a hull test. Runners: they mash inputs through this ending, so check its timing. Sprint 4: the re-mount (B123) is fixed; test the repro above in the test build. B285 (the c_ending4 timing) stays a hypothesis. | test |
| B007 | client.dll, hw.dll, hl.dll | See the causes in the evidence. | The frame rate is bad in general. | Any heavy scene; compare with the toggles named in the cause rows. | User report. Likely causes: the Cg water pass runs every frame even on maps without water (B194); the Paranoia renderer redraws the world once per dynamic light with one draw call per surface (B195); the 100 fps cap without developer (B247); the main thread pinned to CPU 0 (B254); every monster sensing the whole map (B106); the busy-wait limiter (B252); GL state readbacks every frame (B255). | performance | check | Profile one heavy scene pass by pass before changing anything; then skip the water pass where there is no water, batch the light passes, and offer the CPU and cap options as cvars. Runners: the cap and the limiter rows. | open |
| B008 | client.dll | See the causes in the evidence. | The night vision mask makes the frame rate very low. | Turn night vision on and compare the frame rate with gl_dynlight 0. | User report. Likely cause: night vision adds a radius 900 dynamic light every frame, which costs an extra world pass and is duplicated above 100 fps (B198). It also forces a full screen grayscale copy and a second view pass that draws nothing (B199). Night vision also flickers at fps_max 100 (B188). | performance | check | Give the light a fixed key so it never duplicates, skip the empty second view pass, and merge the post effects. Runners: the light's reach must stay the same. Measured 2026-10-28 with rc_prof 2 at the start of c_doc_city (1280x720, the 100 fps cap): with night vision on 1.9 dynamic lights per frame on average and 5.3 ms of work per frame, 3.3 ms of it the light pass; off, 1.8 ms. So the light is almost all of the cost, and at the cap it was drawn about twice per frame. Done: one copy (B198) and no empty second view (B199). Still a full extra pass over the world near the player; batching the world draw (B195) makes that pass cheaper too. Test: the frame rate with night vision on in The Doctor's Story, compared with before. Measured again 2026-10-28 at 1920x1080 in the same spot, uncapped, after the process was moved to the discrete GPU (B296): night vision off 7.00 ms (143 fps), on 8.37 ms (119 fps), so the mask costs 1.37 ms and the light pass alone measures 1.84 ms of it; the grayscale copy is 0.35 ms of GPU and does not show in a CPU bound frame. The user's original report of 40 to 50 fps with the mask was the integrated GPU (B296), not this. What is left of the mask is the light pass, which is B195. | test |
| B009 | hw.dll, hl.dll, client.dll | See the causes in the evidence. | After the three intro videos the game takes about 30 seconds to reach the menu. | Start the game and time from the end of the last video to the menu; +developer 1 +fs_startup_timings 1 prints the precache timings. | User report. Likely causes: the loading picture is drawn with one SetPixel call per pixel, about 2 s at 1080p and 8.6 s at 4K (B257); the 3D menu is a full map load that precaches the whole game (B113); sound init runs twice (B258); more audio devices and a microphone capture open at start (B259); the Cg shaders compile twice on every map load (B192); 70 VGUI images are decoded at the first connect (B200); the in-game advertising code may wait on a dead server (B241). | performance | no | Time each phase with the startup timings, then fix the SetPixel loop and the double sound init first; both are small and safe. Measured on 2026-10-29 by sampling the main thread (dist, 1280x960 windowed, warm file cache): the freeze after the videos was B301, 18 s of Steam's dead crash report servers, then the loading picture (B257); with both out the menu map is up about 2 s after the videos. | open |
| B286 | hw.dll, client.dll | See the causes in the evidence. | The game uses one CPU core and leaves the GPU mostly idle, so players with weak single core CPUs get a low frame rate. | In a heavy scene watch per core CPU load and GPU load (Task Manager or HWiNFO): one core near 100% while the GPU is mostly idle means the frame is CPU bound. | User report. Likely causes, all confirmed in the source: one thread runs the whole frame (B288) and it is pinned to core 0 (B254); the renderers spend CPU time per vertex or per surface instead of handing work to the GPU: models are skinned, lit and sent one vertex at a time (B287), the world is redrawn once per dynamic light with one draw call per surface and every GL call passes through the opengl32.dll wrapper (B195), and GL state readbacks stall the driver's own thread (B255); the Cg water pass (B194) and the busy-wait limiter (B252) add CPU work. MetaRenderer (github.com/MetaHookSv/Renderer, MIT license), a MetaHookSv plugin, replaces the GoldSrc renderer with an OpenGL 4.4 one that draws the world, models and decals from vertex buffers and loads GPU data on worker threads, which shows the approach works on GoldSrc; it does not run with Cry of Fear: its README still lists GoldSrc_CoF 5936 (the 1.6a hw.dll build), but since commit c896688b (2026-10-10) src/plugins.cpp stops with "Renderer does not support Cry of Fear", so it can serve only as a reference (docs/feasibility/cpu_gpu.md). Confidence: likely (each cause is confirmed in source; their share of the frame needs a profiler). | performance | check | Measure first: split one heavy frame into game logic, client, world, models, lights, water, post process and sound. Then let the GPU do the drawing (B254, B255, B195, B287), and after that move work that does not change game state to worker threads (B288). Game logic stays on one thread. Runners: game logic, frame timing and fps_max behavior must stay the same. Step 2 done: rc_prof in client.dll (src/cl_dll/rc_prof.cpp) measures the client phases on CPU and GPU; host_speeds 1 splits the engine part. | open |
| B289 | hl.dll | src/dlls/player.cpp CBasePlayer::PostThink (the m_bRestored block) | In The Doctor's Story the night vision mask comes back on after every level change, also when the player had taken it off. | Doctor's Story: take the mask off with the flashlight key, walk through a level change; the mask is on again in the next map. A save made with the mask off and loaded again does the same. | User report. The m_bRestored block runs after every restore (level transition and save load) and, whenever pev->vuser1.x (doctor mode) is set, sends the HeadShield on message and sets m_iHeadShieldOn = 1 without looking at the restored m_iHeadShieldOn, which is in the save table (DEFINE_FIELD in player.cpp). | gameplay | check | Put the mask back on only when it was on. Runners: in retail the mask is on at the start of every Doctor's Story map, so rc_retail_gameplay 1 keeps that. Done: the mask comes back only when the restored m_iHeadShieldOn is 1; 0 stays off, and a switch still pending at the level change (100 or 101) finishes in the blocks right after it. Test: take the mask off, change level and load a save made with the mask off: it stays off; with the mask on it comes back on as before. | test |
| B290 | hl.dll | src/dlls/monsters.cpp CBaseMonster::SetEyePosition | The crawlers (monster_krypande) do not move, do not react to the player and keep looking the same way. A ReCoF regression from the B085 fix. | The Doctor's Story: c_doc_city, c_doc_sewer2 and c_doc_swift have 5, 3 and 3 krypande; walk up to one. | User report. The B085 fix copied the model's whole eye position into view_ofs. krypande.mdl and krypandenej.mdl store it as 50 0 20 (every other model in cryoffear/models has 0 0 z), and view_ofs is a world offset that does not turn with the monster, so the crawler's eye sat 50 units along the world X axis, often inside a wall, and FVisible never reached the player. | gameplay | no | Take only the height of the model's eye. Done: view_ofs is 0 0 20 for the krypande, the other models keep 0 0 z as before. Test: the crawlers in c_doc_city notice the player and crawl after the player. | test |
| B297 | GameUI.dll | src/gameui/MultiplayerAdvancedDialog.cpp CMultiplayerAdvanced::CreateControls, CMultiplayerAdvancedPage | The Apply button of the options never turns on for the Advanced tab, where Cry of Fear keeps its own settings (the user.scr list: brightness boost, contrast, screen blur, bump and specular maps, water shader, weather, subtitles, grain, vignette and the rest). Only OK applies the Advanced settings, and OK closes the dialog. | Options, Advanced: change any setting; Apply stays grey. | User report ("the Apply button in the options menu never works, it never has"). CreateControls builds `new mpcontrol_t( m_pListPanel, "mpcontrol_t" )` and the CheckButton, TextEntry or ComboBox inside it; EditablePanel::OnChildAdded makes the page the signal target of its direct children only; CMultiplayerAdvancedPage handles no change message and never posts ApplyButtonEnable, which the other pages post (OptionsSubAudio OnControlModified, OptionsSubVideo OnDataChanged, OptionsSubKeyboard). | cosmetic | no | After building the controls the page makes itself their signal target, and a TextChanged or CheckButtonChecked from them posts ApplyButtonEnable. Test: Options, Advanced: Apply is grey when the dialog opens; changing a list, a number or the co-op name turns it on, and pressing it applies the setting with the dialog still open. | fixed |
| B298 | hw.dll | src/engine_hw/vgui_intwrap.cpp CDummyApp; VGUI_EngineSurfaceWrap.cpp AppHandler; vgui2/BaseUISurface.h, BaseUISurface.cpp HasCursorPosFunctions, SurfaceGetCursorPos | With RInput injected (the raw mouse tool many Half-Life players use), the inventory, the padlock, the keypad and the other Cry of Fear menus do not work: hovering and clicking land somewhere else or nowhere. RInput replaces GetCursorPos with a cursor of its own made of raw mouse counts (the last SetCursorPos plus every count since, without acceleration, Windows speed or screen edges), which is what the aim reads; the cursor on screen keeps moving the Windows way. VGUI1's App reads GetCursorPos on every tick and gives the mouse to the panel under that position, and CRECBInventory::MouseOver_guess asks App::getCursorPos which item is under the cursor; vgui2 reads GetCursorPos for GetCursorPosition (the options sliders) because the engine's surface has no cursor functions. | Inject RInput (RInput.exe of VolsandJezuz's RInput Library v1.44) into cof.exe, open the inventory or a padlock and move the cursor over the buttons. | User report ("when injected, the game UI did not work, the inventory, the padlock menus and that"). vgui.dll's App::getCursorPos (export ?getCursorPos@App@vgui@@UAEXAAH0@Z at 0x1001ffc0) calls user32 GetCursorPos, and App::internalTick (0x10002660) calls it through the vtable (+0x34) right before updateMouseFocus (+0xc4) for each surface. vgui2.dll's GetCursorPosition (0x10009570) calls GetCursorPos unless ISurface::HasCursorPosFunctions is true, and BaseUISurface returned false. The window messages carry the real cursor: both VGUI layers already take the position of WM_MOUSEMOVE for the moves themselves. RInput's source (rawinput.cpp, hGetCursorPos and hSetCursorPos) gives this fake cursor to every game outside its list of Source games. | gameplay | no | The engine's App (CDummyApp) overrides getCursorPos and setCursorPos with the position of the last WM_MOUSEMOVE, which the VGUI1 surface keeps, and the VGUI2 surface gets cursor functions that return the same position. Without such a tool it is the position GetCursorPos gives. Test: with RInput injected, the inventory, a padlock and a keypad follow the cursor and the options sliders drag; aiming is RInput's. Without RInput the same menus work as before. | test |
| B302 | client.dll, hl.dll | src/cl_dll/gl_decals.cpp DrawDecals, MsgCustomDecal; src/dlls/player.cpp CSprayCan::Think | Player sprays never show. impulse 201 paints the spray as an engine decal (UTIL_PlayerDecalTrace, TE_PLAYERDECAL), but Cry of Fear's renderer, Paranoia's in client.dll, draws the world and only its own decals (gl_decals.cpp: the customdecal message and the decalinfo.txt groups); nothing draws the engine's decal list. EV_HLDM_DecalGunshot shows it: every bullet hole goes both to R_DecalShoot and to the client's CreateDecal, and only the second one shows. | Bind impulse 201 (Options, Keyboard, Spray logo), face a wall and press it: nothing appears, whatever tempdecal.wad holds, the shipped orange lambda too. | User report ("none of the sprays work"). With a tempdecal.wad the engine accepts, the server takes it (no "Ignoring invalid custom decal") and CSprayCan::Think sends TE_PLAYERDECAL; no client.dll code reads msurface_t::pdecals, and DrawDecals walks only the client's own lists. | visual | no | CSprayCan::Think also sends the customdecal message, named rcspray<player>, for a spray on the world. For the local player the client paints the tempdecal.wad the engine sent (premultiplied, so the see-through parts stay clean), one unit a pixel, upright and not mirrored, lit by the wall's lightmap at that point and by the dynamic lights that reach it (the flashlight among them); 64 sprays at most, cleared with the other decals on a new map. Other players' pictures do not reach the client, so their sprays still do not show; a retail client has no decal group of that name and ignores the message. Test: bind Spray logo, face a wall from close (the spray reaches 128 units), press it: the picture appears, upright and readable; in the dark it is dark and the flashlight lights it. In single player there is no wait between sprays; co-op keeps decalfrequency (30 s). | test |
| B304 | client.dll, GameUI.dll | src/cl_dll/rc_client.cpp RcVsyncFrame, cdll_int.cpp HUD_Frame; src/gameui/OptionsSubVideo.cpp | Vertical sync cannot be turned off in the game. Neither the engine nor the client calls wglSwapIntervalEXT, so the graphics driver's default applies, which is vsync on, and the frame rate is held to the monitor's refresh. The only way out is the driver's control panel. | With developer 1 and fps_max 1000, rc_prof 1 shows the frame held at the monitor's refresh (4.17 ms on a 240 Hz monitor) with most of it in other, the wait for the swap. | User log of 2026-10-29 in c_subtunnel1: frame 4.17 ms (240 fps) in two rc_prof dumps, other 3.3 ms. No qwglSwapIntervalEXT or wglSwapIntervalEXT call in hw.dll, client.dll or opengl32.dll. | performance | no | New client cvar rc_vsync, default 0 (off), archived: HUD_Frame calls RcVsyncFrame, which calls wglSwapIntervalEXT when the value or the GL context changes. Options, Video has a Vertical sync check box for it, which takes effect on Apply without a video restart. A setting forced in the driver's control panel still wins. Test: developer 1, fps_max 1000, rc_prof 1: the frame rate goes past the monitor's refresh with rc_vsync 0 and holds at it with rc_vsync 1; the check box in Options, Video follows the console value and Apply turns on when it is clicked. | test |
| B305 | hw.dll, GameUI.dll | src/engine_hw/sys_sdlwind.cpp CGame::CreateGameWindow_guess; sys_getmodes.cpp (the video mode list and ScreenWindowed); src/gameui/OptionsSubVideo.cpp | There is no borderless window mode. The game runs either in exclusive fullscreen, which switches the monitor's mode and makes alt-tab slow, or in a window with a title bar and borders. Players on widescreen monitors who alt-tab or stream want a borderless window at the desktop resolution. | Options, Video: the display mode offers only fullscreen or windowed. | User request (Sprint 7). The window is created as WS_POPUP, and windowed mode adds WS_OVERLAPPEDWINDOW without the sizing frame (sys_sdlwind.cpp:500-506); the mode comes from the registry values ScreenWidth, ScreenHeight and ScreenWindowed. | cosmetic | no | Sprint 7, investigate first: a borderless choice (a WS_POPUP window at the desktop size, with no display mode change) in the Video options and a launch option, how it interacts with the video mode list, the aspect ratio and FOV code, the mouse clip rectangle and alt-tab, and whether the engine needs a third registry value or can reuse ScreenWindowed. Done: ScreenWindowed 2 in the registry is a borderless window (retail reads any value but 0 as windowed, so the setting stays harmless with retail files). CreateGameWindow leaves out the title bar and borders for it, AdjustWindowForMode then sizes it to the mode and centres it, so at the desktop resolution it covers the screen at 0,0 without a display mode change. The launch option -noborder picks it, -window and the others keep the bordered window. Options, Video has a third display mode, Borderless window, and the engine reports 2 to GameUI (VideoMode_GetCurrentRenderer). Switching while the game runs goes through rc_vidmode (B307). The names are const arrays (.rdata); check_runner kept every LiveSplit and BunnymodXT address. Test: Options, Video, Borderless window at the desktop resolution: the game covers the screen with no border; alt-tab is instant; starting with -noborder does the same; Run in a window brings the title bar back. | test |
| B306 | GameUI.dll, client.dll, hw.dll | src/gameui/OptionsSubVideo.cpp, the Advanced tab (MultiplayerAdvancedDialog.cpp, cryoffear/user.scr); the client and engine code that reads each setting | Every setting should apply at once, without restarting the game. Today some only take effect after the game is restarted. The Video page says so (#GameUI_VideoRestart), and some Cry of Fear settings of the Advanced tab are only read when the renderer or a map starts, so the player has to quit and start the game again to see a change. | Change a setting in Options and look for the change in game without restarting. | User request (Sprint 7). Not yet checked one setting at a time. | cosmetic | no | Sprint 7, investigate first: list every option of the Video, Audio, Advanced and Cry of Fear pages, find for each one where the value is read (every frame, at map start, at renderer start, at engine start), then make the ones that need a restart apply at once, by reloading what they control (textures, shaders, the renderer's cached cvars) or by applying them at the next frame. Checked one option at a time (OptionsSub*.cpp, user.scr): nearly every option is read every frame or on use and already applied at once. Done: the brightness and gamma sliders of the Video page no longer restart the game (retail passed their change on to ApplyVidSettings as a reason to restart, and in single player to save and load, although gl_brightness and gl_gamma rebuild the lightmaps on the next frame); EAX and A3D, read only when the sound device starts, make the Audio page send rc_snd_restart (hw.dll snd_win.c), which starts the device again with the steps of s_disable_a3d through SNDDMA_Init; turning the joystick option on looks for a stick again (IN_JoyMove), so one plugged in after the start works; gl_posteffects, set on the Advanced page, is now archived instead of going back to 1 at every start. Resolution and display mode are B307. Left as they are: a change of sound quality to high affects sounds loaded after it, while those already loaded keep their quality until the next map; gl_picmip, gl_max_size, gl_round_down and texgamma are on no page and act at texture load. cl_vignette on the Advanced page is never read (B308). Test: change brightness and gamma and press Apply in game: no restart, the picture changes; toggle EAX and press Apply: the sound goes on (on today's Windows EAX switches itself back off when the hardware lacks it); plug a pad in, turn Joystick on: it works; set gl_posteffects 0, restart: it stays 0. | test |
| B307 | hw.dll, GameUI.dll | src/gameui/OptionsSubVideo.cpp COptionsSubVideo::OnApplyChanges; src/engine_hw/host_cmd.c Host_SetVideoMode_f, Host_SetRenderer_f; sys_getmodes.cpp VideoMode_SetVideoMode | Changing the resolution, the renderer or windowed mode restarts the whole engine. OnApplyChanges sends _setvideomode or _setrenderer, which only write the registry, and then _restart, so the game in progress is lost and the player waits through the startup again. | Options, Video: pick another resolution and press Apply during a game. | User request (Sprint 7). OptionsSubVideo.cpp:290-298 sends _setvideomode, _setrenderer and _restart; VideoMode_SetVideoMode writes ScreenWidth, ScreenHeight and ScreenBPP and changes nothing else (sys_getmodes.cpp:897). | cosmetic | no | Sprint 7, investigate first: a mode change at run time (resize the window, change the display mode or the borderless window of B305, recreate or resize the GL drawable without losing the context, then rebuild everything sized to the screen: the viewport, the HUD and VGUI layouts, the client's screen sized textures such as blur and post process captures), keeping _restart only for the renderer change. Done (hw.dll host_cmd.c rc_vidmode <width> <height> <0 full screen, 1 window, 2 borderless>): the mode changes in place, keeping the window, its DC and the GL context. CVideoMode_Common::RcSetMode leaves full screen with ChangeDisplaySettings( NULL, 0 ) when a window follows, gives the window the style CreateGameWindow would have given it (SetWindowLong and SWP_FRAMECHANGED) and calls AdjustWindowForMode, the routine alt-tab already uses, which sets the display mode, vid.width and vid.height, the window size and position and the window variables; vid.recalc_refdef is set. The engine then runs the client command rc_vidrebuild (cl_dll rc_client.cpp): it reads the screen info again (ScreenWidth, XRES), builds a new VGUI1 viewport at the new size (its panels take their positions from the screen size when they are built; the old one is taken off the root and hidden but kept, because VGUI1 holds focus and mouse pointers into it), resizes the post process screen texture and the water's screen textures. CHud::VidInit is not called, since it resets level state. GameUI's Video page sends rc_vidmode instead of _setvideomode, _setrenderer and the restart, hides the restart note and keeps open dialogs on the new screen (Taskbar RunFrame). A window is never larger than the desktop, as at start, and the mode must be in the list. Engine code room after B305, B306 and B307: 378 bytes; check_runner kept every LiveSplit and BunnymodXT address. Not refreshed: the engine's VGUI2 EngineFont keeps the size it got for the starting resolution (small text of the engine, cosmetic). Test: in the menu and in a game, change the resolution and the display mode (window, borderless, full screen) and press Apply: no restart, the game reloads from the quick save, the HUD, inventory, notes, subtitles, night vision, water and the options dialog all fit the new size; also from full screen to window and back. | test |

| B312 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/rc_speedrun.cpp RcRunFrame, RcDrawTimer (new); called from HUD_Frame (cdll_int.cpp) | There is no timer in the game. Half-Life runners have BunnymodXT's HUD timer; Cry of Fear runners see their time only in LiveSplit. | Look for a run timer in ReCoF. | User request (2026-11-04): the speedrun features of RebuiltSimon, its HUD the same but with ReCoF and its version in place of RebuiltSimon, the speedometer and jump speed drawn as RebuiltSimon draws them, an in-game timer on the left as Half-Life runners have, and a LiveSplit component as BunnymodXT's BunnySplit. The timing is the LiveSplit script's (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/IlyaTsyganov/Livesplit.CryofFear/main/cryoffear.asl): its gameTime returns nothing, so the time is real time while isLoading is false. isLoading is true while paused (hw.dll +0x11119E4), while a map loads (+0x10D279C is 0), in a cutscene while the player cannot move (client cutscene bars and hw.dll +0x9FDC30), in any cutscene on c_subway2st3 and c_trainscene, while the server map (+0x820495) is c_loadgame, c_difficulty_settings or c_game_menu1, and on the death screen when combase.dll +0x23EB70 is 4072. Unlike BunnymodXT's timer, a pause does not count. | cosmetic | check | Done: rc_hud_timer (default 0) draws at the left edge (rc_hud_timer_pos 0 .5, rc_hud_timer_color 255 255 255) the run time as MM:SS:MS, from an hour on HH:MM:SS:MS, milliseconds in three digits, with the HUD digits and square colons, as the user asked. The timer follows the script rule for rule, from the same values: the hw.dll ones at the offsets ReCoF keeps for the script (tools/check_runner.py fails a build that moves them), the client.dll ones through g_RcAutosplit. It starts when the script starts (the first map of a campaign while it loads, c_rumpel1, c_hallo_iambulletproof and c_arvuti once the cutscene bars are gone, or the co-op start music), resets when the script resets (a start map whose decal counter drops to 0, the co-op countdown, c_intro), counts real time (QueryPerformanceCounter) on the frames where isLoading is false, and stops at the endings the script splits on (endmusic1 to 4, coopend, manhunt, lifelover, survive_hotel_terror and collab_csong, or c_doc_ending). A frame counts only when the one before counted too, so a load that blocks between frames never counts, and a frame longer than a second adds nothing. Left out: the combase.dll test (data of a Windows file, it differs between Windows builds), so on the death screen the timer counts. The script samples about 60 times a second and the timer every frame, so the two can differ by a frame or so at each pause, load or cutscene. Runners: check against LiveSplit over a whole run before using it for anything. Second request (2026-11-04): LiveSplit's timer must follow the game's, worked by hand as BunnymodXT's (bind DEL "map of1a1;bxt_timer_reset;bxt_timer_start;..." in Half-Life). Done: rc_timer_start (starts from zero after a reset, or goes on with a stopped run), rc_timer_stop and rc_timer_reset; rc_timer_auto 1 (the default) keeps the script's start, reset and end, and a run that has counted nothing is not reset, so the bind "map c_nightmare;rc_timer_reset;rc_timer_start" does not start twice when the decal counter drops during the load. The state is published in g_RcRunTimer, a block of its own found by the tag ReCoF_RUNTIMER (docs/speedrun.md), for the autosplitter of B313. Test: rc_hud_timer 1, start a new game: 00:00:000 until control comes, then it runs; pause, loads and cutscenes stop it; past an hour it shows the hours; the bind above resets and starts it; rc_timer_stop and rc_timer_start stop and resume it; it matches LiveSplit's game time. First in-game test (2026-11-05, user screenshot): the run started but the timer stayed at 00:00:000 on c_nightmare while the player moved. Cause: the script's hw.dll values only happen to follow the game's state in retail. Their fields, from the hw.dll map and the struct layouts of the 1.6a compiler: "loading" (+0x10D279C) is cl_lightstyle[21].map[4..7], the text of light style 21; "can move" (+0x9FDC30) is g_svmove.physents[26].name[0..3], the name of the 27th physics entity; "paused" (+0x11119E4) is a field of playerstate[15] in cl.frames[0]. In ReCoF one of them said loading or paused all the time. Fix: the timer reads the real state: paused is the engine's cl.paused (ref_params paused, taken in V_CalcRefdef); loading is no map, the decal counter at 0, or no view drawn since CHud::VidInit; frozen is FL_FROZEN or no speed in the client's player move; dead is no health; the maps and menus are the level name. The script starts and resets while a start map loads, when no game frame runs; the timer does it on the first frame that map is drawn. g_RcRunTimer goes to version 2 and carries this state for the autosplitter. rc_timer_debug prints it. Second test (2026-11-05): rc_timer_debug showed loading 1 while playing. Cause: the decal counter was always 0. Retail's code at the script's client addresses (disassembled from client.dll 1.6a) shows +0x1BE920 is lastDrawTime (glows.cpp, written by DrawGlows every frame, set to 0 by ResetGlows in CHud::VidInit) and +0x5440C8 is the start of READ_STRING's buffer, while g_RcAutosplit pointed at decaltextures[0] + 8 (bytes 8 to 11 of the name "shot", always 0) and at the buffer + 0x58, from wrong names in the old retail address map. g_RcAutosplit now points at lastDrawTime and at the buffer, which also fixes the script addition of docs/speedrun.md. Third test (2026-11-05): the timer ran, but c_nightmare to c_start did not split. Dead now comes from the engine's client data (the view's ref_params health), since the HUD's number keeps the last map's value until the server sends one, and the autosplitter's rules wait for the run's first map: a run started by rc_timer_start in a bind sees the menu or no map first, which the script's load and death test takes for a save being loaded, turning map splits off. Fourth test (2026-11-05), with ReCoFSplit.log: the published map went c_nightmare, (none), c_start: the level name is empty for a frame during a level change, and the script's test (map empty while loading) turned the splits off. The script's map is a name in the engine's model cache that keeps the last map through a level change, so g_RcRunTimer's map is now the last map loaded, and only loading looks at the level name itself. User request (2026-11-11): the timer must start only with rc_timer_start, not whenever a campaign's first map loads. Done: the automatic start and reset are gone, and rc_timer_auto with them; the run still stops by itself at the endings, as BunnymodXT's timer stops at the end of Half-Life. | test |

The split files of the categories (from the speedrun.com resources: Any%, All Key Items NMG, Any% chapters, Any% Co-op, Manhunt, The Doctor's Story, Memories, Halloween, Community Collaboration, The Stairway) use the script's settings Split on map change, Split on chapter change, Undo when loading the savefile and Water glitch. | cosmetic | yes | In progress: the component is the ReCoF Autosplitter, kept private for now and not in this repository. LiveSplit's Game Time, start and reset come from the game's run timer (B312, g_RcRunTimer), so LiveSplit and the HUD timer agree. It reads cof.exe's memory as the script does (hw.dll at the offsets ReCoF keeps, client.dll through g_RcAutosplit), about 60 times a second, and splits by the script's rules, ported one for one (map change with its exclusions, water glitch, Lake Skip, chapters, undo of the splits made since the last save on a load or a death); an ending splits once instead of on every update. The settings page has BunnySplit's layout (the user's screenshot): Enable AutoSplit, Split on game end, the rules, and Enable AutoStart and AutoReset; instead of typing maps, a category list (New Game / New Game+ map or chapter splits, All Key Items + NMG, The Doctor's Story, Co-op, Manhunt, Memories, Halloween and Community Collaboration, The Stairway) sets the rules from that category's split file and lists its splits. Its tests replay the five categories whose splits are named by maps (New Game 93, All Key Items 95, Co-op 30, Manhunt 12, The Doctor's Story 8): each splits once per segment. The settings live in the layout, so split files do not change. Test: copy LiveSplit.ReCoFSplit.dll into LiveSplit's Components, add Control, ReCoF Autosplitter to the layout, turn off cryoffear.asl, pick the category, compare against Game Time and play: LiveSplit starts, splits and shows the game's time. | open |
| B314 | client.dll, hw.dll | src/cl_dll/rc_crash.cpp (new); input.cpp InitInput; cdll_int.cpp HUD_Frame; src/engine_hw/sys_dll2.cpp CEngineAPI::Run, MiniDumpFunction_guess | Most crashes leave the game frozen on its last frame instead of closing with an error. The engine (sys_dll2.cpp) and the client (InitInput) register Steam's breakpad crash handler, which writes a minidump into Steam\dumps without telling the player and leaves the window as it was; and nothing notices a game that stops running frames, so a hang looks the same. | Any crash in game, or a hang (an endless loop or a deadlock). | User report (2026-11-10): "most crashes are the game freezing, nothing more". The 11 cof.exe dumps in Steam\dumps (2026-11-03 and 11-05) came from the Steam install, not ReCoF; the two opened crash inside RebuiltSimonCoFManager, and the player saw a frozen game. The engine's own fatal errors (Sys_Error, sys_dll.c) already close the window and show a message box. | crash | no | Done: rc_crash.cpp, installed by InitInput right after SteamAPI_Init so its unhandled exception filter replaces breakpad's, and kept in place by a watchdog thread. A crash hands its exception to a reporter thread made at start-up (the crashed thread may have no stack left), which writes crashes\crash-<date>_<time>.dmp (MiniDumpWriteDump, with the referenced memory) and .txt next to cof.exe, puts the desktop's display mode back and the cursor free, shows the exception, the place as module+offset, the stack (StackWalk64, and the code addresses found on the stack for code without frame pointers) and the file names in a message box (Ctrl+C copies its text, Yes opens the folder), and ends the process. The report also lists the registers, every module with its link time stamp (the exact build), the map and the last lines of qconsole.log. A hang is no client frame (HUD_Frame) for 30 s while the window stopped answering (IsHungAppWindow, so a long map load that still answers and the engine's own error box are left alone): the watchdog writes hang-<date>_<time>.dmp and .txt with the main thread's stack, and asks whether to close the game (No keeps waiting). -rc_nocrashhandler leaves the retail behavior; rc_crashtest 1 crashes and rc_crashtest 2 hangs on purpose. Checked outside the game with a test program built from the same file: both reports and dumps written, the dump opens in cdb on the crashing instruction. Not covered: a crash before the client starts (engine start-up keeps breakpad). Test: rc_crashtest 1, then rc_crashtest 2 after a restart: a message box each time, files in crashes\. First in-game test (2026-11-10): rc_crashtest 2 showed its report, rc_crashtest 1 did not, and Steam\dumps got crash_cof.exe_20261110213157_1.dmp. Its stack: client.dll (the test) under Cbuf_Execute and Host_Frame, then hw.dll's __CxxFrameHandler, _CallSETranslator, MiniDumpFunction_guess and steamclient's Breakpad_SteamWriteMiniDumpUsingExceptionInfoWithBuildId, waiting in crashhandler.dll. CEngineAPI::Run sets _set_se_translator( MiniDumpFunction_guess ) and wraps RunListenServer in try/catch(...), so every crash of the main thread, most of them, went to Steam before any unhandled exception filter; hw.dll links its C runtime statically, so the client cannot change that translator. Fix: MiniDumpFunction_guess first calls client.dll's exported RcCrash_Report when client.dll has it (GetModuleHandle and GetProcAddress, the two names as const arrays in .rdata); it takes the crash when the reports are on and returns to Steam's handler with -rc_nocrashhandler. hw.dll code room 378 to 314 bytes, .rdata 2084 to 2052; check_runner kept every LiveSplit and BunnymodXT address. Checked with the test program doing what Run does (an SE translator inside try/catch(...)): the report took the crash. Confirmed in game (2026-11-10): the user's rc_crashtest 1 now shows its report too, as rc_crashtest 2 did. | fixed |
| B315 | client.dll, hw.dll | src/cl_dll/rc_smooth.cpp (new); view.cpp V_CalcRefdef, V_CalcNormalRefdef, CalcViewModelLag_guess; inputw32.cpp IN_SmoothMouseMove; cdll_int.cpp HUD_Frame, HUD_Redraw; vgui_TeamFortressViewport.cpp; HUDControl_guess.cpp; hud_redraw.cpp; glows.cpp; gl_renderer.cpp; particlesys.cpp; tri.cpp; geiger.cpp; src/engine_hw/host.c _Host_Frame; CL_TENT.C CL_FxBlend; net_ws.c NET_DrawTextFields | The game draws one picture per game frame, and the game runs at most 100 frames a second (Host_FilterTime caps fps_max at 100 without developer), so on a monitor faster than 100 Hz the view turns and moves in 10 ms steps. Raising the cap is not an answer: it changes the movement and the timing of the game. | On a 144 Hz or faster monitor, turn with the mouse and strafe: the picture changes 100 times a second. | User request (2026-11-10): frame extrapolation, as a Half-Life project and Geometry Dash have, so the game looks like more frames at the same game frames, and cl_showfps showing the frames drawn and the game's rate. _Host_Frame returns at once while Host_FilterTime says the next frame is not due, so that time is idle (the B252 sleep). Three audits of the render path (the engine under SCR_UpdateScreen, the client's 3D view, the client's HUD and menus) listed what changes on every draw rather than with time. | cosmetic | check | Done: smooth frames, rc_smoothframes (default 1) and rc_smoothframes_fps (default 0, the monitor's refresh rate). hw.dll's _Host_Frame calls client.dll's exported RcSmooth_Frame (GetProcAddress on the client module, the name a const array in .rdata) on each pass of its loop while the next frame is not due, with SCR_UpdateScreen (not Host_UpdateScreen, so startmovie records only the frames), &cl.time and &host_frametime. RcSmooth_Frame splits each game frame evenly into at least as many parts as the monitor shows (240 Hz: two pictures in each 10 ms frame, 300 a second; 144 Hz: one; 100 Hz or less: none) and draws a picture only when it should end before the next frame is due, judged from the slowest recent picture plus 0.5 ms, so the frames should keep their timing and the msec of the commands. Nothing of the game runs in a picture: cl.time stays, cl.oldtime is set to cl.time and host_frametime to 0 around it, so particles, screen shakes, the center print, studio events and the view's punch, bob and sway do not step, and the HUD's clock sees no time pass. Two things move: the mouse turns the view at once (IN_SmoothMouseMove is the IN_MouseMove of the next frame when the mouse only turns the view, so that frame sends the sum of the same mouse counts), and in the player's own first person view (not the wheelchair, boat, telescope, attached, spectator tank or entity cameras, third person or a dead player) the camera and the weapon models move ahead by the player's velocity for the time since the frame's picture, stopped by the player's hull at walls, floors and monsters (PM_TraceLine). The weapon lag and the stair step smoothing go on for that time, so they meet the next frame. Monsters, doors and animations stay at the frame's state. What changed on every draw keeps its speed in pictures: the menus' solve (TeamFortressViewport::solveTraverse; every client panel is under the viewport), the hint bar, gun smoke and the other render fx fades (engine CL_FxBlend), rain splashes and drips, Geiger clicks, muzzle flashes, the net graph's fps; glows, skybox sprites and particles on the weapon model are drawn in pictures too (their lists are filled by HUD_AddEntity in frames only). No pictures while paused, in intermission or while a demo plays or records. cl_showfps shows "300 fps (100 logic)" while pictures are drawn; the engine's counter is left out of those frames through the cvar's value, and its text, which the config saves, is not touched. Left as a higher fps_max would: beam noise and the random flicker of some lights roll once per picture, and per-draw random numbers (the engine's, shared with the server) run more often, so tool-assisted runs that need the same random numbers want rc_smoothframes 0. hw.dll code room 314 to 186 bytes, .rdata 2052 to 2036; check_runner kept every LiveSplit and BunnymodXT address. Test: a 144 Hz or faster monitor, rc_vsync 0, cl_showfps 1: about 300 fps (100 logic) at 240 Hz, 200 at 144 Hz; turning and strafing look smooth; the weapon does not jitter while turning, and the view does not jump when landing from a jump, walking into a wall, going up stairs or riding a lift; menus, notes, the inventory, the credits and the hint bar keep their speed; rain, gun smoke, lamps' glows, skybox sprites and muzzle flashes look as before; rc_smoothframes 0 brings back the 100 fps picture. First in-game test (2026-11-10): the counter showed both rates at first (the menu map), but once the new game's first map (c_nightmare) had loaded, no more pictures came. Likely cause: the slowest recent picture was forgotten only when a picture was drawn, so one slow picture (the first ones after a load are) stopped them for good. Fix: it is forgotten with time (half in about a third of a second), a picture longer than a game frame counts as one game frame, the estimate is never below the game frame's own picture (from its view to the end of its HUD, measured every frame), and a picture that still ends after the next game frame was due stops them for 0.25 s, doubled up to 8 s when it happens again within 10 s. rc_smoothframes_debug prints the last second: game frames, pictures, why pictures were left out (off, no game view, paused or demo, monitor too slow, no time before the next game frame, pausing after a late one) and the longest game logic, game frame picture and picture in between. Test again: new game, c_nightmare and the next maps keep the two rates; where they stop, rc_smoothframes_debug says why. Second test (2026-11-10): no pictures at all, not even on the menu map. Likely cause: the new lower bound, the game frame's own picture, is no guide to a picture in between: it also sets up the models' new poses and the lights, which the pictures reuse, so it took most of the time left and every picture was left out. Fix: no such bound; a picture is taken to need the slowest picture of the last half second (forgotten with time, as before), or 2 ms when none was drawn in it. With rc_smoothframes on, cl_showfps now always shows the two rates (100 fps (100 logic) when no picture fits), and rc_smoothframes_debug also prints the time left when each game frame was over. Third test (2026-11-10), with rc_smoothframes_debug on the menu map: target 1, no picture in any game frame (88 paused by the menu, 12 "monitor too slow"); 3.9 to 5.9 ms left after each game frame, its own picture 3.1 ms at most. The user had set rc_smoothframes_fps to 1, taking it for a switch, so the pictures aimed at 1 a second, and that may also be why the second test showed none. Fix: rc_smoothframes_fps counts only above the game frames' rate; 0 or anything up to it (1 among them) follows the monitor. Fourth test (2026-11-10, rc_smoothframes_debug in c_start and c_start2): monitor 240 Hz found, 165 to 191 pictures a second with 6 to 8 ms left after each game frame and pictures of 1.3 to 1.9 ms, but at times none for whole seconds: one picture of 3.87 ms ended late, and the pause after a late picture (0.25 s, doubled up to 8 s within 10 s) then left out every picture, 200 in one second with 6 to 8 ms left in every frame. The user also finds the UI slower, as if lagging (not explained yet). Fix: no pause; a picture needs its estimate plus a quarter of it and 0.5 ms, and a late picture adds the time it overran to that margin (at most half a game frame), forgotten by 1 % per game frame. User decision (2026-11-11): it works, but oddly at times, so it is experimental and off by default (rc_smoothframes 0); the launcher's list and the release notes say so. | test |
| B316 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/rc_discord.cpp (new); src/external/discord-rpc (new: YaLTeR's fork of discord-rpc at db64a0a, MIT) and src/external/rapidjson (new: RapidJSON 1.1.0 headers, MIT); cdll_int.cpp HUD_Frame; input.cpp HUD_Shutdown | Discord shows nothing of the game itself: the ReCoF Launcher set a presence with the version only, and only while the launcher was open. | Play with Discord open: the profile shows no map. | User request (2026-11-11): the presence in the game rather than in the launcher, as BunnymodXT does with discord-rpc (https://github.com/YaLTeR/discord-rpc/tree/db64a0a71635130f10654b487c717702af52a886), showing ReCoF, the map and so on. The Discord application is the launcher's (id 1564718095418003586). | cosmetic | no | Done: rc_discord (default 1). The details say ReCoF and its version; the state says "On <map>", "In the menus" on the menu maps or with no map, or "Co-op on <map> (n of m)" with more than one player slot; the time shown runs from the game's start; the large image is the application's asset "recof", if it has one; a button "Get ReCoF" opens the GitHub page. It is sent only when it changes. discord-rpc's own thread keeps the connection and connects when Discord starts later; HUD_Shutdown and rc_discord 0 clear it. The launcher sets no presence any more (ReCoF-Launcher b2509f6). Test: Discord open, start ReCoF: the profile shows ReCoF, its version and "In the menus"; on a map "On <map>" and the time; rc_discord 0 takes it away. User request after the first look (2026-11-11), with two screenshots of BunnymodXT's card: the same layout, and discord-rpc as a submodule as BunnymodXT has it. Done: the card reads ReCoF (the application's name), then "Map: <map> | Version <version>" (in the menus only the version), then "Playing" (in co-op "Playing co-op (n of m)") or "In the menu", "FPS: <frames drawn each second>" and the difficulty (Easy, Normal, Hard, Nightmare, from the difficulty cvar the server sets); the menus are the engine's own, with no map loaded, and the 3D menu maps. A new place, state or difficulty is sent at once, the FPS alone every 15 s. discord-rpc (YaLTeR's fork at db64a0a) and RapidJSON (v1.1.0) are git submodules in src/external instead of copied files (git submodule update --init). | test |
| B317 | client.dll, hl.dll | src/dlls/rc_cheats.cpp (new), client.cpp ClientCommand and SetupVisibility, game.cpp, triggers.cpp CChangeLevel::ChangeLevelNow; src/cl_dll/rc_practice.cpp (new), gl_renderer.cpp DrawBump and RecursiveDrawWorld, vgui_TeamFortressViewport.cpp paintTraverse | ReCoF has none of BunnymodXT's practice and routing tools: setting health, position, velocity and view angles, seeing through walls, the whole map and far entities, keeping level changes from starting, hiding the VGUI. | Look for bxt_ch_set_pos or a wallhack in ReCoF. | User request (2026-11-11), with BunnymodXT's descriptions (its wiki, Console commands and variables): the bxt_ch_* commands, bxt_set_angles, bxt_novis, bxt_render_far_entities, bxt_wallhack with _additive and _alpha, bxt_disable_changelevel and the bxt_disable_* that apply to Cry of Fear, leaving out what is Half-Life only and the HUD parts ReCoF already has. | cosmetic | check | Done, with rc_ in place of bxt_: rc_ch_set_health, rc_ch_set_pos, rc_ch_set_pos_offset, rc_ch_get_pos (it also prints a line that puts the position and the view back), rc_ch_set_vel, rc_ch_set_vel_angles <magnitude> or <pitch> <yaw> <magnitude>, and rc_set_angles <pitch> <yaw> [roll] (the server sets them through fixangle), all needing sv_cheats 1 as BunnymodXT's do; the client sends them to the server, which runs them for the player who typed them. rc_render_far_entities and rc_novis (server cvars, sv_cheats 1) send every entity of the map (SetupVisibility gives no PVS, which the engine's visibility test passes); rc_novis also turns the engine's r_novis on, which the client sets and puts back. rc_wallhack (sv_cheats 1, the ReCoF renderer, gl_renderer 1) draws the world's surfaces in a pass of their own after the opaque ones, textured without lightmaps, blended at rc_wallhack_alpha (0.6) or added with rc_wallhack_additive 1 and without writing depth, so models, sprites and brush entities behind walls show; world decals are left out while it is on. rc_disable_changelevel (no sv_cheats) keeps trigger_changelevel from starting. rc_disable_vgui hides every VGUI panel (notes, subtitles, menus, the HUD parts VGUI draws) while the view and the client's own HUD stay. Left out: bxt_ch_set_armor (Cry of Fear has no armor), bxt_disable_hud (Cry of Fear has hud_draw), bxt_disable_nightvision_sprite (Opposing Force and CZ Deleted Scenes only) and bxt_disable_autosave (Cry of Fear's trigger_autosave does nothing). Unlike BunnymodXT's bxt_novis, rc_novis does not change what monsters see. Test: sv_cheats 1; rc_ch_get_pos, then rc_ch_set_pos with its output; rc_ch_set_vel_angles 1000; rc_set_angles 0 90; rc_wallhack 1 with monsters behind a wall, with rc_wallhack_alpha 0.3 and rc_wallhack_additive 1; rc_novis 1; rc_render_far_entities 1; rc_disable_changelevel 1 at a level change; rc_disable_vgui 1 with a note or subtitles on screen; with sv_cheats 0 the commands say they need it. First in-game test (2026-11-11): rc_ch_get_pos printed both lines; rc_ch_set_angles was unknown (BunnymodXT's is bxt_set_angles, so rc_set_angles); rc_ch_set_vel_angles 1000 showed no push on the ground; the console offered none of the commands while typing, since only the server knew them; and BunnymodXT's bxt_show_triggers was missing. Done: the client registers the practice commands too and hands each one to the server, so the console completes them; rc_ch_set_angles is another name for rc_set_angles; rc_show_triggers (server cvar, sv_cheats 1) sends the triggers, which InitTrigger keeps from being drawn, see-through (render mode trans color, amount 70) and coloured by what they do: trigger_changelevel green, trigger_teleport blue, trigger_hurt red, trigger_push yellow, trigger_once and trigger_multiple magenta, the rest orange. Not explained yet: the velocity commands on the ground (the movement has no cap on velocity there and ground friction is 4, so the push should show); to check in the air. | test |
| B318 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/mp3.cpp CMP3::PlaySFX_guess; rc_client.cpp RcWeaponSoundSpeed (new) | The pitch of the player's own weapon sounds changes at every shot, so replacement gun sounds (for example the ones of Cry of Fear 1.5 put into 1.6a) sound wrong. | Fire a weapon several times and listen to the pitch, with the 1.6a sounds or with replacements in sound/weapons_new. | User request (2026-11-11, from a player who wants the 1.5 gun sounds in 1.6a; the user chose the name). In 1.6a the server sends the player's weapon sounds as PlaySFX messages (util.cpp PlayWeaponSound_guess), and the client plays them through irrKlang from sound/weapons_new (the first folder of weapons/<folder>/<file> becomes weapons_new) with setPlaybackSpeed( RandomFloat( -0.1, 0.1 ) + 1.0 ), so each shot plays 0.9 to 1.1 times as fast and its pitch moves with it. The other players' weapon sounds in co-op go through the engine at PITCH_NORM. | audio | no | Done: rc_omgbruh_sound (client, archived, default 0 as retail); 1 plays every shot at its recorded speed and pitch. Also in the launcher's options (Other). Test: rc_omgbruh_sound 1 and fire a few shots: the same pitch every time; back to 0: it varies again. | test |

| B086 | hl.dll | Save/Restore of CCheckBrush, CFuncValve, CCoFLadderManagerUse, CStatueUse, CCoFCoopAllPlayersButton, CAnimatedCameraModel, CPasswordNote, CCofInterDoorOnOff, CCoFMDLCutscene | `IMPLEMENT_SAVERESTORE( CClass, CBaseEntity )` although the classes derive from CBaseToggle, CBaseAnimating or CBaseMonster: the fields of the base classes in between are not saved (confirmed in the 1.55 debug build). | Save and load near these entities: their base class state is lost. | the macro base (DeCoF original_bugs.md) | save | no | Not by changing the base in IMPLEMENT_SAVERESTORE: a retail save has no tables for the classes in between, so the first intermediate ReadFields finds this class's header, rewinds and returns 0, and the generated Restore stops before reading this class's own fields (saverestore.h IMPLEMENT_SAVERESTORE, util.cpp CRestore::ReadFields). Write a Restore per class that reads the intermediate tables only when their header is there, then its own table, and a Save that writes the full chain. Done with IMPLEMENT_SAVERESTORE_RC (saverestore.h) on the nine classes: Save writes the real base chain, and Restore goes on when the base chain stops at a table a retail save does not have, so retail saves load exactly as before and new saves keep the base class state (toggle state, delays, animation, and for the monster based ones CBaseMonster's own reset after a load). A ReCoF save loaded in retail loses these nine classes' own fields. | test |

A second, unpatched co-op memory bug. Weapons without primary ammo (switchblade, axe, nightstick: pszAmmo1 NULL, so m_iPrimaryAmmoType is -1) are carried with `m_rgAmmo[m_iPrimaryAmmoType]`, i.e. m_rgAmmo[-1]. In CBasePlayer the member just before m_rgAmmo is m_pStoredActionItem, so the carry copies the old level's m_pStoredActionItem pointer into the new player; if it was not NULL, the new player holds a pointer into the freed previous level, dereferenced when an action ends (swim or punch restore) and it also blocks the no-weapon punch branch. | co-op, change level through an inter_door_coop door carrying a melee weapon while m_pStoredActionItem is set (for example right after a punch or a swim whose restore failed), then end an action on the new map; expect a crash or a wrong weapon. | SetPlayerVariables `playervariablestruct[i].iAmmo1 = pPlayer->m_rgAmmo[((CBasePlayerWeapon *)pPlayerItem)->m_iPrimaryAmmoType];` and GivePlayerVariables `pPlayer->m_rgAmmo[((CBasePlayerWeapon *)pPlayerItem)->m_iPrimaryAmmoType] = playervariablestruct[i].iAmmo1;` (same for weapons 2 to 6); player.cpp GetAmmoIndex `if (!psz) return -1;`; player.h `CBasePlayerItem *m_pStoredActionItem; int m_rgAmmo[MAX_AMMO_SLOTS];`; player.cpp action end `if ( m_pStoredActionItem && HasNamedPlayerItem( STRING( m_pStoredActionItem->pev->classname ) ) )`. BuildPlayerList writes the same out-of-bounds value into txtfiles/<name>.cfg, but its reader (ApplyCFGSettingToPlayer) only restores weapons with pszAmmo1. The 256-byte line copies in ApplyCFGSettingToPlayer are already listed in docs/original_bugs.md. Source: s1_gameplay.md. | crash | no | skip the clip and ammo copy when m_iPrimaryAmmoType is negative (both directions) and clear m_pStoredActionItem on the new player after the carry. | test |

The carry file is `<gamedir>/txtfiles/<netname>.cfg` with the raw player name, and the engine name cleanup does not remove "/" or ".", so a client named for example "../config" makes the host write its state file outside txtfiles (cryoffear/config.cfg overwritten with health, stamina and weapon lines). | co-op, join with the name ../test and go through an inter_door_coop level change; a test.cfg appears in cryoffear instead of cryoffear/txtfiles. | BuildPlayerList `strcat( filename, "/txtfiles/" ); strcat( filename, STRING( pPlayer->pev->netname ) ); strcat( filename, ".cfg" ); std::ofstream os( filename );`; engine_hw/sv_main.c SV_ExtractFromUserinfo only trims spaces, leading "#", "console" and duplicates. Source: s1_gameplay.md. | latent | no | build the file name from the user id or a sanitized name (letters, digits, underscore) and keep it inside txtfiles. | open |

When the final fight ends, cof_stopwheelchair calls Disembark, which puts the player at the chair origin plus 36 units (inside the chair's box) and restores `SetUse( &CWheelchair :: WheelchairUse )`. A USE press near the chair right after that mounts it again: WheelchairUse only checks that m_pPlayer is NULL. From then on PreThink re-freezes the player every frame, the client view is forced onto the chair (hiding the ending trigger_cameras), and the map has no second cof_stopwheelchair; only the credits cof_mdlcutscene (ckamera1, 165 s into multiending5) calls StopWheelchairMode again, so the ending looks frozen for minutes (softlock). | c_ending4b, kill the last book Simon; about 6 s later (multi_manager fuckoff: stoooop at 6, global14 at 6.5) control returns on the chair; look down about 45 degrees and press USE: the chair view and freeze come back while the ending cameras run. | Disembark `m_pPlayer->pev->origin = pev->origin; m_pPlayer->pev->origin.z += 36; ... m_pPlayer->m_pWheelchair = NULL; m_pPlayer = NULL; pExitEntity = NULL; SetUse( &CWheelchair :: WheelchairUse );`; WheelchairUse `if ( !m_pPlayer ) { ... m_pPlayer->EnableControl( FALSE ); Embark(); }`; PlayerUse: the direct trace skips the chair because CBaseEntity::Create made the player its owner (engine_hw/world.c SV_ClipToLinks owner test), but the sphere search (PLAYER_SEARCH_RADIUS 80) has no owner test, and with the idle bbox of models/player_sick.mdl the clamped line of sight from the eye is straight down, so the VIEW_FIELD_NARROW (0.7) test passes at about 45 degrees down; PlayerUse is not blocked by FL_FROZEN; PreThink `if ( m_pWheelchair && !( pev->flags & FL_FROZEN ) ) EnableControl( FALSE );`; view.cpp replaces vieworg and viewangles with the chair while iWheelchair_guess is set. Map: the cof_teleport fitttth (6.1, 7, 8 s) receive the monster_generic justhealth as activator (CBaseMonster trigger fires with `this`, multi_manager passes m_hActivator), so CCofTeleport::Use moves nobody and Simon stays next to the chair; trigger_once *61 (master multiscr14) covers the whole arena and starts multiending5 even with a frozen player; cof_stopwheelchair is the only stop before ckamera1. Source: s1_gameplay.md. Confidence: likely (mechanism confirmed in source and map; the exact input reporters use needs an in-game check). | gameplay | yes | in Disembark keep WheelchairUse off for this ending (or refuse Use from the player who just left), and put the player on a free spot next to the chair with a hull test instead of inside it. Runners: runners mash inputs through this ending; the fix only removes the bad re-mount, but routes that touch the ending timing should be checked. Done in CBasePlayer::StopWheelchairMode: after a scripted stop the chair's Use is turned off. Only c_ending4b has a wheelchair (made by cof_wheelchairmode, stopped by its one cof_stopwheelchair; no map has a wheelchair_exit, the path where Disembark re-arming Use matters), so nothing else changes. The player is still placed at the chair origin as in retail, so the ending looks the same. | test |

| B181 | client.dll, hw.dll | src/cl_dll/TextureManager_guess.cpp LevelInit_guess, CreateTexture_guess; src/cl_dll/CgWater_guess.cpp Init_guess; src/cl_dll/PBuffer_guess.cpp; src/cl_dll/hud_msg.cpp MsgFunc_InitHUD; src/engine_hw/GL_DRAW.C GL_LoadTexture | Client texture manager uses fixed GL texture names from 2000 that collide with the engine's texture numbers. On every InitHUD (map load and save load) the client resets its texture counter to 2000 and binds names 2000 and up for the water scene, mask, offset and distort textures and the pbuffer, filling them with glCopyTexImage2D; the engine hands out GL names sequentially from 1 and never reuses the names of non-world textures, so once a session has loaded enough models and sprites the two ranges overlap: world or decal textures show screen captures, and the water post effect samples engine textures (white or garbled screen). | Play 2 to 3 hours across many maps; floors, windows or bullet holes start showing the player's view or wrong textures; restarting the game clears it. | LevelInit_guess sets m_iNextTexture_guess = 2000 and CreateTexture_guess does *texture = m_iNextTexture_guess++ then glBindTexture and glCopyTexImage2D. The engine starts texture_extension_number_guess at 1 (GL_VIDNT.C) and GL_LoadTexture gives each new slot texture_extension_number_guess++; non-world textures keep servercount 0 and are never unloaded (GL_UnloadTextures only frees servercount > 0), so the counter only grows; MAX_GLTEXTURES is 4800, so it can pass 2000. The Paranoia renderer (1<<25) and VGUI (5810) use ranges the engine cannot reach. The overlap is certain; that a normal session reaches 2000 is not measured: printing texture_extension_number_guess at each InitHUD would confirm it. Confidence: likely. | visual | yes | Allocate the texture manager's names with glGenTextures (or a reserved high base) and delete them on level init instead of reusing 2000 and up. Runners: the "save, restart, reload" workaround costs time in runs; the fix does not touch gameplay. | open |

| B195 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/gl_renderer.cpp DrawBump, RendererFilterEntities; gl_light_dynamic.cpp DrawDynamicLights, RecursiveDrawWorldLight, LightDrawPoly; gl_rsurf.cpp DrawPolyFromArray, SetupTexMatrix, RenderSecondPass, RenderSpecular | Paranoia renderer redraws visible world surfaces once per dynamic light with per surface draw calls. The world is drawn in several passes (first pass, additional bump pass, one pass per active dynamic light, second pass, specular), each surface as its own glDrawArrays(GL_POLYGON) call, and each light pass walks the BSP again and loads a texture matrix and binds a normal map per surface. Cry of Fear creates many lights every frame: flashlight variants (keys -1, -2, -8, -66), entity lights (renderfx 71, 72, 73, 165, 68, 245, 184) and muzzle flashes. | In a lamp heavy area compare FPS with gl_dynlight 0, gl_specular 0 and gl_bump 0 one at a time; FPS should track the number of lights in view. | DrawBump calls RecursiveDrawWorld, RenderAdditionalBumpPass, DrawDynamicLights (loops MAX_DLIGHTS 32), RenderSecondPass, RenderSpecular; LightDrawPoly does Bind2DTexture, SetupTexMatrix (glMatrixMode(GL_TEXTURE) and glLoadMatrixf) and DrawPolyFromArray per surface; GL_POLYGON makes the driver triangulate on the CPU. The opengl32.dll wrapper (src/opengl32) adds one pass-through call per GL call, which adds up with this many calls. Source: s1_perf.md. Confidence: likely (structure confirmed in source; its share of frame time needs a profiler). | performance | no | Batch surfaces per texture and per light into triangle index buffers built at load, cull light passes with per light surface lists, and later move lighting to shaders in one pass. Step 1 done (rc_worldbatch, default 1): between RcBatchBegin and RcBatchEnd, DrawPolyFromArray queues each polygon as a fan of triangles in an index list, and the list goes to the driver in one glDrawElements when a state helper (Bind2DTexture, SetTexEnvs, SetTexPointer, SetTextureMatrix, the light texture matrix) is about to change something, or when the scope ends. Scopes: the world first pass (RecursiveDrawWorld), the first pass of brush entities, every texture chain of the second pass, the extra bump passes, and the world part of each dynamic light pass, where SetupTexMatrix skips a matrix equal to the one loaded so polygons of one plane and texture axis stay together. Same polygons, same order, same state, so depth tests and coplanar surfaces resolve as before; rc_worldbatch 0 draws one call per polygon again. rc_prof counts the calls as world draws. Next steps: per light surface lists instead of a BSP walk per light, then lighting in shaders. Test: rc_prof 1 shows far fewer world draws with rc_worldbatch 1 than 0 in the same spot, the frame time drops on a CPU bound machine, and the picture is identical: walls, bump mapping, detail textures, the flashlight and lamps, night vision, glass and other see-through brushes. Second step done (rc_lightshader, default 1): one pass dynamic lights on the world go through a vertex shader (gl_light_dynamic.cpp). The retail pass loaded a texture matrix on unit 0 for every surface (SetupTexMatrix, the vector to the light in the surface's tangent space), which split the batches of step 1 at every new plane; the shader works out the same vector per vertex from the face's tangent space, now kept per vertex in an array beside the world vertex buffer (gl_rsurf.cpp RcTangentArrays), and the eye linear coordinates of units 2 and 3 from the planes the setup functions give GL. Only the vertex stage changes: the cube map, dot3, attenuation, spot and colour stages and the fog stay fixed function, and the position uses ftransform so the depth matches the first pass for GL_EQUAL. The light passes also stop walking the BSP once per light: the visible nodes with surfaces are gathered once per view, each light tests them against its box or cone (a node's box holds its children's, so the same nodes pass as in the walk), and the surfaces it reaches are sorted by normal map before drawing, which is allowed because one pass lights add with GL_ONE, GL_ONE where the depth is equal, so the order does not change the result. Each light then needs about one draw per normal map. Two pass lights (gl_twopassdyn 1, or fewer than four texture units), moved brush entities and drivers without OpenGL 2.0 keep the retail path. Combining several lights in one pass was left out: it needs a fragment shader, which would have to copy the combiners and both fog modes the game uses (linear from env_fog, exp2 from the engine). Test: rc_prof 1 in a lamp heavy area shows far fewer world draws with rc_lightshader 1 than 0, and the lit walls look the same: the flashlight and its cone, lamps, muzzle flashes, night vision, bump mapping, fog areas, and lights near the edge of their radius. Confirmed in game by the user on 2026-11-02 (rc_prof dumps with the cvar on and off, picture the same). | fixed |
