| B292 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/gl_rsurf.cpp SetTextureMatrix, SetupTexMatrix, DrawBumpedPoly, RenderSecondPass, RenderAdditionalBumpPass; gl_light_dynamic.cpp LightDrawPoly | The texture matrix is reloaded for every surface although it almost never changes. SetTextureMatrix keeps no state, so each call runs glActiveTextureARB, glMatrixMode(GL_TEXTURE), glLoadIdentity, an optional glScalef and glMatrixMode(GL_MODELVIEW). DrawBumpedPoly calls SetTextureMatrix(2, 0, 0) for every bumped surface of the first pass, loading the identity the pass already has, and RenderAdditionalBumpPass loads the same identity again right after the pass. RenderSecondPass calls it once per surface of a texture chain whose scale is the same for the whole chain. The light pass has the same defect in SetupTexMatrix: the matrix it loads really does differ per surface, but the glActiveTextureARB(GL_TEXTURE0_ARB) and glMatrixMode(GL_TEXTURE) in front of the glLoadMatrixf do not, and nothing inside the loop sets the mode back, so two of its three GL calls are repeated for every lit surface. | bump_debug 1 shows how many surfaces are drawn bumped; each of them costs four extra GL calls per frame, all through the opengl32.dll wrapper, and every lit surface of every dynamic light costs two more. | SetTextureMatrix holds no static state, unlike Bind2DTexture (currentbinds), SetTexEnvs (envstates) and SetTexPointer (texpointer), which all cache and skip the call when nothing changed. DrawBumpedPoly calls it in the MAX_TU 4 and MAX_TU 3 branches with both scales 0, so only the identity load remains. Measured 2026-10-28 in c_doc_city at 1920x1080 on an RTX 3050: bump_debug counts 4840 world polygons in view, 3747 of them bumped and 1093 normal, plus 468 high quality specular, so the world costs about 9000 draw calls per frame before the light pass; world 1st is 2.35 ms and one dynamic light is 1.84 ms. The identity loads are 3747 times four GL calls per frame, roughly 15000 calls that change nothing. Confidence: confirmed in source, its exact share of world 1st still has to be measured by removing it. | performance | no | Give the texture matrix the same per unit cache the other state helpers have, keyed on the unit and the two scales, and clear it in ResetCache; track the active texture unit and the matrix mode the same way so SetupTexMatrix issues the glLoadMatrixf alone. The identity loads then collapse to one per pass, the second pass loads one matrix per texture chain instead of one per surface, and the light pass drops two of its three matrix calls per surface. The picture does not change. This is the cheapest frame time in the renderer: it is local, it needs no new data and it carries no look risk. Done: gl_rsurf.cpp now keeps the matrix of each unit (texmatrix) and which unit is selected with the matrix mode already on GL_TEXTURE (texmatrixsel), next to the bind, texture environment and pointer caches. SetTextureMatrix returns at once when the unit already holds what is asked for, so the per surface identity loads of DrawBumpedPoly and the per surface detail scale of RenderSecondPass collapse to one load per pass, and SetupTexMatrix and SetupTexMatrix_Reflected keep their glLoadMatrixf and drop the two calls in front of it while the light pass stays on unit 0. Bind2DTexture and SetTexEnvs forget the selection when they move the active unit, ResetCache forgets everything, and every place that loads a texture matrix without the helpers calls RcTexMatrixDirty: the specular reset, the light projection, DisableProjection, FinishSpotLight, FinishPointLight and both ends of GL_MtexStateSaver. Test: the picture is unchanged in a lit area of c_doc_city with bumped surfaces and a dynamic light in view, detail textured maps still show their detail, and rc_prof shows a lower world 1st and dlights time in the same spot. | test |
| B293 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/gl_rsurf.cpp RenderSecondPass | The second pass repeats the per texture work for every surface of a chain. Inside the while loop that walks one texture's surface chain it calls GetExtTexData(tex[i]), SetTexEnvs, SetTextureMatrix and Bind2DTexture, and all four depend only on tex[i], not on the surface. Everything except the draw call belongs above the loop. The pass also walks all world->numtextures entries every frame to find the few with a chain. | rc_prof 2: the world 2nd scope grows with the number of surfaces rather than with the number of textures, and most of all while a dynamic light is alive and every visible surface is in a chain (B291). | the body of the while loop in RenderSecondPass; Bind2DTexture and SetTexEnvs are cached and cheap, SetTextureMatrix is not (B292), so a detail textured chain pays four or five GL calls per surface. Confidence: confirmed in source; cost unmeasured. | performance | no | Hoist GetExtTexData, SetTexEnvs, SetTextureMatrix and the unit 1 bind above the while loop, and keep a list of the textures that have a chain instead of scanning them all. B195 replaces the whole pass with material batches later. The picture does not change. Done for the hoisting: GetExtTexData, SetTexEnvs, SetTextureMatrix and the unit 1 bind are now set once per texture chain, above the while loop that walks its surfaces, which draws exactly the same thing because none of them depends on the surface. With B292 in, the three helpers were already skipping their GL calls, so what this removes is the call and branch overhead per surface, not GL traffic; the list of non empty chains is left to B195, which replaces the pass. Test: the picture is unchanged on a detail textured map and on c_doc_city, and rc_prof shows the same or a lower world 2nd time. | test |
| B294 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/gl_studiomodel.cpp CStudioModelRenderer::Lighting, GL_DrawPoints (the per mesh normal loop) | The per normal lighting function carries the dual weapon attachment work, and it runs for meshes whose result is thrown away. Lighting is called once per normal of every mesh of every visible model. Every call saves m_pCurrentEntity, tests g_iDualLighting_guess, the HIDEHUD_WEAPONS flag and g_pHeadShieldEnt_guess and restores the pointer; when the test passes it also runs StudioCalcAttachments, IEngineStudio.StudioClientEvents, gEngfuncs.GetEntityByIndex and a memcpy, which belong to the model and not to the normal. The same loop lights the normals of meshes drawn with a fullbright texture, where the colour comes from cof_fullbrighttextures and the computed value is discarded. | rc_prof 2 in a scene with many models: the models scope grows with the total number of normals rather than with the triangles actually shaded. | GL_DrawPoints loops j over nummesh and i over pmesh[j].numnorms and calls Lighting for each; Lighting ends with the dual weapon block; the draw loop picks bFullbrightTex once per mesh (B287 step 1) and then uses cv_fullbrighttextures_guess->value instead of g_pvlightvalues. Confidence: confirmed in source; its share of the 2.69 ms models scope measured on 2026-10-28 in c_doc_city needs the sub scopes the profiler does not have yet. | performance | check | Run the dual weapon block once per model where the weapon is armed instead of inside the per normal function, and skip the lighting loop for meshes whose texture is fullbright. Both also make the loop pure, which is what a worker thread (B288, docs/feasibility/multicore.md) or a vertex shader (B287 step 3) needs. Runners: the block feeds the left hand weapon's muzzle flash and light, so the attachments must be produced at the same point of the frame and the events must still fire once. Done: the dual weapon block runs once in GL_DrawPoints, before the normal loop of the first submodel that has normals after the flag is set, which is where retail's first Lighting call ran it, so the attachments come from the same bones at the same point of the frame and the events fire once; Lighting no longer saves, tests and restores per normal. Meshes with a fullbright texture skip Lighting while cof_fullbrighttextures is not 0, except fullbright_mess with the phone off, the one case whose colour reads the light values; chrome coordinates are still worked out for them. Test: the left hand weapon of a dual wield (the flashlight in the left hand, a dual pistol) fires its muzzle flash and lights as before; glowing textures (phone screen, coronas) look as before. | test |
| B295 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/gl_rsurf.cpp PrepareFirstPass, ResetCache; gl_renderer.cpp DrawBump, R_DrawBrushModel, RecursiveDrawWorld; gl_light_dynamic.cpp RecursiveDrawWorldLight | The world model is fetched from the engine inside both BSP recursions, and the first pass setup is repeated per brush entity. RecursiveDrawWorld and RecursiveDrawWorldLight call gEngfuncs.GetEntityByIndex(0)->model at every node that holds surfaces, so a frame makes thousands of calls across the DLL boundary for a pointer that does not change, once for the world walk and again for every dynamic light. R_DrawBrushModel also calls PrepareFirstPass for every moved brush entity it draws, which throws away the bind, texture environment and texture pointer caches through ResetCache and walks all world->numtextures textures to null their chains, and DrawBump clears the same chains again at the end of the frame. | a street or corridor with several moving brush entities (doors, trains, platforms) in view: the world 1st and brush ents scopes of rc_prof 2 grow with the number of moved entities rather than with their surfaces. | R_DrawBrushModel runs PrepareFirstPass whenever onlyfirstpass is false, which is the moved entity loop of DrawBump; PrepareFirstPass ends with the loop over world->numtextures; DrawBump repeats that loop after the decals; RecursiveDrawWorld and RecursiveDrawWorldLight both fetch the world model inside the recursion. Confidence: confirmed in source; cost unmeasured. | performance | no | Done for the recursions: DrawBump stores the world model in g_RcWorldModel once per frame and both walks read it from there. The rest is not as simple as this row first said and is left open with its reasons. The per entity PrepareFirstPass cannot just be hoisted: a moved brush entity runs its own RenderSecondPass and RenderSpecular, so its texture chains have to be empty first, or it would redraw the whole world's chain; only the ResetCache inside it looks removable, and that depends on whether DrawHighQualitySpecularChain binds textures behind the caches, which has not been checked. The duplicate clear at the end of DrawBump is worth a few hundred pointer writes a frame and leaving the chains filled between frames is the kind of thing that turns into a dangling pointer on a map change, so it stays. While looking at this, a second thing turned up: the moved entity path never calls RenderAdditionalBumpPass, so the lightmap chains a moved bumped brush entity fills are never drawn. That is a separate visual issue and needs its own row once it is reproduced. Test: the picture is unchanged, and the world and dlights scopes of rc_prof are the same or lower. | test |

When the main thread is inside Windows or a driver, a buffer swap or a driver wait, it stops only where the kernel lets it, after up to 12 ms on the laptop, so slow frames in the driver were written up as the system holding the processors: the perf log blamed drivers or the BIOS for waits of the game's own frame. | Run rc_perflog 1 where the swap is slow (a laptop with two graphics chips): STUTTER lines say the system held the processors although a separate test program sees no gap then. | Laptop tests (2026-11-15): the stalls gathered at the slowest spots of a map tour (1.5 to 2 a second there, 0.03 a second over 9249 s). | performance | no | Done: the late wake is timed from the Sleep call itself; each sample keeps how long the main thread took to stop, and its line under the STUTTER adds "stopped after N ms, inside Windows or a driver" from 1 ms; the header explains the at lines. On the rerun of c_the_stairway3, c_the_stairway2 and c_down the laptop logged no system stall and two samples stopped after 12 ms. | fixed |
| B351 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/rc_crash.cpp RcReporterThread, RcWatchdogThread, RcCrash_Init, RcCrash_Shutdown; src/cl_dll/inputw32.cpp IN_Shutdown; src/cl_dll/rc_client.h | The crash and hang reports of B314 start two threads that were never ended: the hang watchdog sleeps a second at a time and the reporter waits for a crash, and the unhandled exception filter stays pointed at client.dll. When the game quits the engine unloads client.dll before the process ends, and a watchdog that woke in between ran into the unloaded code: an access violation at RcWatchdogThread+0x2b in client.dll_unloaded, which Windows logs as a crash of cof.exe (and which Steam's crash handler, called for it, could turn into a 0xC0000409 crash of crashhandler.dll). | Quit the game many times: about one quit in ten crashed on the way out. | Laptop tests (2026-11-15): Windows' Application log held five faults at RcWatchdogThread+0x2b in two hours of map tours. | crash | no | Done: a quit event (manual reset) ends both threads: the watchdog waits on it for its second, the reporter waits on it with the crash event. RcCrash_Shutdown, called from IN_Shutdown after the perf log's and the bone threads' shutdowns, sets it, waits up to 2 s for the threads and puts back the filter that was there before ours; if a hang report's message box keeps the watchdog, client.dll is pinned so that it is never unloaded under it. On the laptop 4 of 15 quits with the perf log on crashed before the fix (exit code 0xC0000005, client.dll_unloaded) and none of 25 after it, every exit code 0. | fixed |
| B352 | hw.dll | src/engine_hw/sys_sdlwind.cpp CGame::WindowProc_guess (WM_CLOSE) | Closing the game window (its X button, Alt+F4, or Windows closing it) set the engine to quit at once, so the main loop ended without the quit command's CL_Disconnect and Host_ShutdownServer. Host_Shutdown then unloaded everything with the local server and its Steam game server never shut down (Steam_Shutdown is only called from Host_ShutdownServer), and as the process ended Steam's crashhandler.dll stopped it with 0xC0000409 (FAST_FAIL_FATAL_APP_EXIT): a crash logged by Windows every time the window was closed, in retail Cry of Fear as well. Calling Host_ShutdownServer from Host_Shutdown instead was tried and crashed: the menu, shown by the disconnect, started its music from VGui_Shutdown after CDAudio_Shutdown had ended the music thread. | Close the game window with a map loaded: the process exits with 0xC0000409 and Windows' Application log names crashhandler.dll. | Laptop tests (2026-11-15, i3-7020U with Steam): 6 of 6 window closes crashed with ReCoF and 2 of 2 with retail Cry of Fear; a disconnect before the close made it exit cleanly. | crash | yes | Done: WM_CLOSE adds the quit command to the command buffer, as the menu's Quit does, so the next frame quits the same way as typing quit, while the engine is active as before. On the laptop 4 of 4 window closes with a map and 2 of 2 from the main menu exited with code 0 and no fault; quit is unchanged. | fixed |
| B353 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/rc_prof.cpp RcStallThread, RcStallDescribe, RcStallWrite | The perf log named a STUTTER's samples on the main thread when it wrote the line: up to six samples of 1024 stack words each, and Windows was asked about every word that might be code (VirtualQuery). On a slow computer with an AMD driver that took 10 to 40 ms, as the query waits while a driver's thread changes the process's memory; the time went into the next frame, which then stuttered as well, so a log showed chains of stutters the game did not have, and the player felt them while the log ran. Keeping Windows' answers for the regions met (fewer questions) and reading the module list once a stutter instead were both tried and were not enough: the module snapshot itself took 10 to 30 ms there. | rc_perflog 1 on a slow computer, in a place with many monsters: STUTTER lines come in chains a few frames apart, the later ones with most of their time in other, and their samples show the main thread inside RcStallWrite. | i3-7020U laptop with a Radeon R7 M440 (2026-11-15), c_stad toured the same way: 21 stutters, 15 of them right after another, and 39 samples inside the log's own writing. | performance | no | Done: the sampler thread names each sample as soon as it has let the main thread go on (Windows' loader is never asked while that thread is stopped), keeping what Windows said of each region met in that sample sorted by address, and the main thread only prints the prepared text under the STUTTER line. The same tour: 10 stutters, 2 right after another, and no sample inside the log; the log's lines read as before. | fixed |
| B354 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/gl_renderer.cpp R_DrawBrushModel; gl_rsurf.cpp GenerateVertexArray, RcFaceOutsideView | A brush entity whose box touched the view was drawn whole: every face turned to the camera, with its texture binds and draw calls, whether in view or behind the player. Half of the 240 maps hold more of their faces in brush entities than in the world (93 have one of 1000 faces or more), as Cry of Fear builds much of its scenery as func_illusionary and func_wall: c_stad has one func_illusionary of 3536 faces over the whole map and c_buscity 13510 faces in brush entities, more than twice its world. The same spots: c_stad 68 fps (world 1st 5.5 ms), c_buscity 86 fps (4.7 ms); paused screenshots in three directions are identical pixel for pixel with it on and off. | fixed |
| B355 | client.dll | src/cl_dll/gl_renderer.cpp DrawBump, RecursiveDrawWorld, R_DrawBrushModel, RcWorldSortAdd, RcWorldSortDraw | The world's first pass drew each face as the BSP walk met it, and a static brush entity's faces in the entity's order. A profile of the main thread there on an i3 laptop had 64% of its time inside AMD's OpenGL driver (atioglxx.dll), most of it under the batches' glDrawElements, spread over the driver's code rather than in one copy: the cost of each draw with four texture units bound (bump mapping), which turning bump maps off cut to a third. | c_stad at -835 620 -76 facing 270 with rc_prof 2 on a slow computer: world 1st is most of the frame. | i3-7020U laptop with a Radeon R7 M440 (2026-11-15), uncapped, with B354: c_stad 67 fps (world 1st 5.6 ms), c_buscity 85 fps (4.9 ms). | performance | yes | Done: rc_worldsort 1 (default) keeps the faces of the walk and of the static brush entities drawn with it (rendermode normal) and draws them at the end of the first pass, sorted by a key of the entity's turn (0 for the world), bumped or not, the texture and the lightmap page; each keeps its entity for R_TextureAnimation, and an alpha tested entity draws the faces kept before it first. The world comes before the entities and they keep their order, so equal depths resolve as before: paused screenshots of c_stad and c_buscity in eight directions are identical pixel for pixel (an earlier form that mixed the entities with the world differed by one pixel where one met a wall). The same spots: c_stad 87 fps (world 1st 2.6 ms), c_buscity 105 fps (2.7 ms); on a laptop with an i5-13420H and an RTX 3050 the same spots went from 231 to 243 fps and from 294 to 311 fps | fixed |

The jump pushes off at 270 units a second, 2.7 units a frame at 100 fps: it took three frames of jump to get out, and a jump of one frame (a mouse wheel jump, a quick tap) left the player inside, where the ladder stopped them and the hold pulled them back to the column. Getting on from the front needs the player at the ladder face, where the use brushes stop them. The box, the column and the hold are unchanged, so the boxes of saved games keep working, and the far side and the width still count the whole hull, so getting on from the top floor and from the sides is as before. PlayMP3 stops the old track at once as before and hands the new one to a thread of its own, which creates it paused; the next CMP3::Frame takes it over, sets its volume and starts it, so a track starts a few frames later. A track asked for while the game was not paused and taken over while it is stays paused; one whose fade out ended meanwhile, or one a newer track or a stop replaced, is let go. IrrKlangDestroy_guess ends the thread before the sound engine goes. rc_music_thread 0 creates the track on the main thread as before. Built; not tested in game. | test |

| B510 | client.dll, opengl32.dll | src/cl_dll/rc_vr.cpp, rc_vr.h (new); src/cl_dll/view.cpp (V_CalcRefdef), input.cpp (CL_CreateMove), inputw32.cpp (RcIN_MouseFree), hud_redraw.cpp (CHud::Redraw), hud_update.cpp (fov), gl_renderer.cpp (PrepareRenderer), StudioModelRenderer.cpp (StudioSetupBones), GameStudioModelRenderer.cpp (R_StudioDrawModel), rc_smooth.cpp, rc_client.cpp; src/opengl32/opengl32.cpp (glFrustum, glViewport, wglSwapBuffers), opengl32.def; src/external/openvr (OpenVR SDK 2.15.6, BSD-3); tools/make_dist.py, tools/dist_extra/Play ReCoF VR.bat | Cry of Fear has no VR. The user wants a VR version that normal players never see: only with a launch option, with hands, the inventory and the menus usable, to try with a Quest 3S (2026-11-21). | Start SteamVR (a Quest through Steam Link, Quest Link or Virtual Desktop), then Play ReCoF VR.bat (cof.exe ... -vr). | Research in the vault (vr.md) and I:\COFPP\reports\ReCoF VR version research.md: the engine's own pass loop (hw.dll V_RenderView repeats V_CalcRefdef and R_RenderView while ref_params.nextView is set) can draw each eye with no hw.dll change. | feature | no | Done on branch openvr: with -vr (or -openvr) client.dll loads openvr_api.dll next to cof.exe and starts SteamVR; without it nothing loads and every hook returns at once (the archived settings in config.cfg are not touched). Each frame the game's view runs once, then four engine passes draw the left eye, its head shield, the right eye and its head shield into two framebuffers; opengl32.dll gives each eye the headset's own asymmetric projection (glFrustum in projection mode) and a viewport over the whole eye; the second eye counts as a render-only picture (RcSmooth_RenderOnly) and fires no model events; the engine and the client renderer cull with a field of view wide enough for an eye. The head moves the eyes from the game's eye position (lean clamped to rc_vr_lean units, world scale rc_vr_scale units a metre), a camera turns the world by its own yaw while the head looks round in it. The right controller holds the weapon model: its wrist bone (r_wr) is pinned to the controller's grip point and the arm bones are folded into the wrists (rc_vr_hidearms), so the hands float with the gun; the left hand item (flashlight, lantern) goes on the left controller. The server shoots from the eye at the point the right controller points at (a trace), so shots go where the gun points. Left stick moves (relative to the head, or the left hand with rc_vr_movehand 1), right stick snap turns (rc_vr_snapturn, 0 for smooth), jumps and crouches; triggers fire and aim, grips use and dodge, A reloads, B the next quick slot, X the inventory, Y the menu, left stick click sprints, both grips held recentre. The HUD, menus, inventory and console are copied from the window at the swap onto a screen in front of the player (head-locked HUD with its dark parts see-through, a fixed panel for menus) with a laser pointer from the right hand that moves the cursor and clicks with the trigger. The left eye and the 2D go to the desktop window. Not done yet: per-eye post effects (grayscale, blur and the water copy work on the window, not the eyes), engine particles move twice a frame, shots start at the eye, melee by swinging, a wrist HUD, body holsters. Untested in a headset. | test |

Done in hw.dll: pushMakeCurrent pushes the projection matrix and popMakeCurrent pops it, instead of reading it back with glGetFloatv and loading it again, for every VGUI panel painted (the HUD and the menus paint many panels a frame, so this was the readback that repeated). Panels do not nest their push and pop, so the stack needs one level above the view's projection. check_runner: every LiveSplit and BunnymodXT address kept, .bss unmoved. The other readbacks stay, by decision: R_SetupGL reads the two matrices the engine then uses (r_world_matrix and the projection for WorldToScreen), GL_MtexStateSaver reads its 14 values once per frame in one place and needs the active texture unit the engine left, which it cannot know otherwise, and the post process reads only while grayscale (night vision) is on. Test: the HUD, the inventory, the notes and the menus draw in the right place in game and in the main menu, also after alt-tab and with the console open. Confirmed in game by the user on 2026-11-02 (rc_prof dumps with the cvar on and off, picture the same). | fixed |
| B287 | client.dll, hw.dll | src/cl_dll/gl_studiomodel.cpp CStudioModelRenderer::GL_DrawPoints (the default path, gl_renderer 1 and gl_modelrenderer 1, chosen in StudioModelRenderer.cpp StudioRenderFinal_Hardware); fallback src/engine_hw/r_studio.c R_GLStudioDrawPoints, R_GLStudioDrawPointsATI_guess, R_StudioTransformAuxVert, R_StudioLighting | Models are skinned, lit and drawn on the CPU one vertex at a time. Every frame, for every visible model, each vertex is moved by its bone matrix and lit on the CPU, then the triangles go to the driver with glBegin(GL_TRIANGLE_STRIP or GL_TRIANGLE_FAN) and one glVertex3f call per vertex. Cry of Fear's own Paranoia model path in client.dll does this by default; the engine's path, used when gl_renderer or gl_modelrenderer is 0, works the same way. The cost grows with the polygons in view and sits on the main thread while the GPU waits; neither path uses vertex buffers for models. | A scene with many monsters or detailed models: frame time should grow with the number of models in view while GPU load stays low. | GL_DrawPoints transforms every vertex with VectorTransform (gl_studiomodel.cpp line 1580) and submits it with gl.glBegin and gl.glVertex3f (lines 1869 to 2095); StudioRenderFinal_Hardware calls it when cv_renderer and cv_nsr are set (StudioModelRenderer.cpp line 2161), both registered with the default "1" (gl_renderer.cpp lines 1257 and 1274). The engine fallback R_GLStudioDrawPoints calls R_StudioTransformAuxVert for every vertex (r_studio.c line 4015) and qglBegin and qglVertex3f per vertex (lines 4136 to 4221). Source: source review for B286, corrected by docs/feasibility/cpu_gpu.md. Confidence: likely (structure confirmed; its share of frame time needs a profiler). | performance | no | Upload each model's vertices once to a vertex buffer and do bone skinning and lighting in a vertex shader, starting with the client's GL_DrawPoints; keep the CPU path as a fallback for old GPUs. Hit boxes and attachments keep using the CPU bone matrices, so game logic sees the same results. First step done: GL_DrawPoints no longer runs string tests per vertex. The texture name tests (fullbright, fullbright_mess, corona_, sleeve, glove), the view model test and the hand fade scale are made once per mesh, and the dual weapon and severed limb bone name tests (up to 4 strstr and 22 strcmp per vertex) once per bone (RcHideBone); the per vertex random brightness of corona textures stays per vertex, so the engine random sequence is unchanged. Same picture; with cof_fullbrighttextures at its default 0.5 every vertex of every model used to pay for a strstr and a GetViewModel call. The GPU path is the next step. Second step done: each strip goes to the driver as one glDrawArrays from a client vertex array (texture coordinate, colour, position) instead of three GL calls per vertex through the opengl32.dll wrapper, which GL defines to draw the same thing; the cvar rc_modelarrays (default 1) turns it off, and the world pass and strips over 4096 vertices keep immediate mode. The CPU still skins and lights each vertex: that part is the GPU step. Test: models look the same with rc_modelarrays 1 and 0, and rc_prof shows less models time with 1. Third step done: the strips and fans of one mesh share all their state, so RcEnd turns each one into the triangles GL would draw from it (strip triangle k from k, k+1, k+2, or k+1, k, k+2 for odd k, fan triangle k from 0, k+1, k+2: same winding, same last vertex) and adds them to one list, and RcMeshFlush draws the list in one glDrawArrays(GL_TRIANGLES) at the end of the mesh, and before an immediate mode strip so the drawing order is kept. A model goes from one call per strip to one call per mesh, which is where the laptop measurements put most of the models time (driver submission). The GPU path (skinning and lighting in a vertex shader) stays the next step. Test: models look the same with rc_modelarrays 1 and 0, including see-through and additive parts, the hands, dual weapons and severed limbs, and rc_prof shows less models time with 1. Fourth step done (rc_gpumodels, default 1): the vertices of each submodel go to a vertex buffer once per map, in model space with the bone of each position and each normal (strips and fans turned into the same triangles as step 3), and a vertex shader (src/cl_dll/rc_gpumodel.cpp, entry points in rc_glsl.cpp) moves them by the bone matrices and lights them as Lighting does: ambient, the lambert term with the light direction, the four dynamic lights per bone with their distance falloff and spot cone (GL_SetupLighting now records where each light's vectors start, its radius and its cone), flat shaded meshes, cof_fullbrighttextures and the hand fade. The normal is turned into world space and dotted with the world vectors, the same product as the CPU's dot with vectors turned into the bone's space. The fragment stage stays fixed function, so texture environments, register combiners, blending and alpha test are unchanged; the last vertex's colour and texture coordinate are set afterwards as immediate mode left them. The CPU path stays for meshes with chrome coordinates or the random corona brightness (it uses the engine's random sequence), for submodels with a hidden hand or severed limb (those skip vertices inside strips), inside the world pass, and for every model when the driver lacks OpenGL 2.0 or 1700 vertex uniform components (the console log says which). The bone hiding test moved up in GL_DrawPoints so the choice is made before the vertices are moved; it now counts only bones that are really hidden, which skips the same vertices. rc_prof counts the meshes drawn this way as gpu meshes, and mdl vertices and mdl lighting drop to nearly nothing for them. Test: rc_prof 1 shows gpu meshes above 0; models look the same with rc_gpumodels 1 and 0 (monsters, the hands and both dual pistols, the flashlight and muzzle flash lighting on monsters and hands, night vision, see-through and glowing parts, fullbright screens and the phone, the hand fade after a cutscene, sky models, severed limbs); the models time drops. The engine's own fallback, R_GLStudioDrawPoints for gl_renderer 0 or gl_modelrenderer 0, stays as it was. Confirmed in game by the user on 2026-11-02 (rc_prof dumps with the cvar on and off, picture the same). | fixed |

The only other threads are networking (NET_ThreadMain_guess), HTTP downloads, the CD audio worker (thread.cpp) and irrKlang's own thread for the client's music. | Watch per core load in a heavy scene: one core near 100% while the others idle. | _Host_Frame calls SV_Frame (host.c line 1231) and ClientDLL_Frame (1248), then Host_UpdateScreen runs SCR_UpdateScreen (1090) and Host_UpdateSounds runs S_Update (1152); CreateThread appears only in net_ws.c line 1000, download.cpp line 525 and thread.cpp line 129. ## opengl32.dll

| ID | Binary | Where | Problem | Repro | Evidence | Severity | Runners | Fix plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B273 | opengl32.dll | src/opengl32/OtherOGL.cpp Init, sys_glGetString; src/opengl32/opengl32.cpp DllMain; src/cl_dll/glmanager.cpp (PARANOIA_HACKS_V1 check); src/cl_dll/gl_renderer.cpp | opengl32.dll wrapper refuses to load if any system export is missing, and the renderer needs it. The wrapper resolves about 350 GL and wgl entry points from the system opengl32.dll and returns FALSE from DllMain if a single one is missing (Wine lacks the undocumented wglGetDefaultProcAddress); without the wrapper the client does not see PARANOIA_HACKS_V1 and turns the custom renderer off, so the flashlight, dynamic lights and hands break (Proton default, ReShade). | Proton without WINEDLLOVERRIDES="opengl32.dll=n,b", or ReShade's opengl32.dll: no flashlight, extra hands; Proton users also need +gl_twopassdyn 1 for dynamic lights. | each GetProcAddress failure in Init returns FALSE (wglGetDefaultProcAddress included) and DllMain returns Init(); sys_glGetString appends " PARANOIA_HACKS_V1"; gl_renderer.cpp prints "No paranoia hacked opengl32.dll, custom renderer disabled" and sets gl_renderer 0. The single-pass light path that needs gl_twopassdyn 1 on Mesa (gl_light_dynamic.cpp SetupSpotLight, SetupPointLight with 4 texture units) was not checked on Windows drivers. Source: https://github.com/ValveSoftware/Proton/issues/2379 ; https://github.com/hinqiwame/cof-linux-patcher ("Wine may lack the wglGetDefaultProcAddress function") ; https://reshade.me/forum/troubleshooting/7892-cry-of-fear-texture-corruption-issue (replacing opengl32.dll breaks the flashlight and lanterns) ; https://www.protondb.com/app/223710. | visual | no | Make missing optional exports non-fatal, and move the hacks into client.dll (call the real GL directly and detect support itself) so no proxy DLL is required; consider falling back to two-pass lights when the single pass is not supported. | open |

The 32-bit process is limited to 2 GB of address space, which the 128 MB hunk, the OpenGL driver's copies of the large Paranoia textures and malloc'd file loads exhaust; loads then fail with "COM_LoadFile: not enough space for models/weapons/mobile/v_mobile.mdl" (also player.mdl, chainsawguy2.mdl) or "not enough memory", or textures turn black or blocky before a crash. | Chapter 1, the door right after the padlock (first map transition); or any long session (2 h and more), crash on a door loading screen. The community "64-bit crash patch" (cof.exe patched for a 4 GB address space, per PCGW and the Linux patcher, which calls it the official Team Psykskallar crash patch) fixes most cases. | versions/1.6a/cof.exe and dist/cof.exe have FileHeader.Characteristics 0x010F, without IMAGE_FILE_LARGE_ADDRESS_AWARE (0x20). COM_LoadFile raises exactly this Sys_Error when the usehunk 5 Mem_Malloc returns NULL. Sys_InitMemory caps the hunk at 0x8000000 (128 MB) whatever -heapsize says (CoFLaunchApp passes -heapsize 1536000). The Paranoia normal, gloss and detail textures are cached by name for the whole session (ExtTextureData and DetailTexture lists, freed only in RendererCleanup), and CCgWater_guess::Init_guess and CTextureManager_guess::CheckMipmapSupport_guess strdup the GL extension string on every InitHUD without freeing it. The exact v_mobile.mdl load path of the 2013 report was not found in 1.6a (the only full-.mdl COM_LoadFileForMe is R_StudioReloadSkin in src/engine_hw/r_studio.c). Source: https://steamcommunity.com/app/223710/discussions/0/828937979394022620 ; https://steamcommunity.com/app/223710/discussions/0/864969481834233284 ; https://steamcommunity.com/app/223710/discussions/0/2930112548436454174 ; https://steamcommunity.com/app/223710/discussions/0/3877093659889991475 ; https://www.pcgamingwiki.com/wiki/Cry_of_Fear (Crashing on Door Transition Load Screens) ; https://github.com/hinqiwame/cof-linux-patcher. | crash | yes | Link cof.exe with /LARGEADDRESSAWARE and audit code that compares or stores pointers as signed int (PCGW says the 4 GB patch can mute some sound effects until a reload, which points at such code, possibly in Miles or the sound cache); free the Paranoia texture cache entries not used by the new map and the strdup buffers. Done: cof.exe links with /largeaddressaware, one header byte against the baseline, the same flag the community 4 GB patch sets (the maintainer's Steam cof.exe already carries it). A choice made in Windows Settings, Graphics still wins, which is the way back to the integrated GPU; offering it in the ReCoF Launcher is left to that repository. ## Adding an issue

Add a row to the section of its binary, or to "User reports" when it comes from a player and the cause is not known yet, with the next free ID. Check the problem against the source before it goes in, and say in the evidence when it could not be checked. When a user report turns out to have a cause listed elsewhere, link that row's ID in the evidence instead of moving the report.

## Crash dumps

Retail's crashhandler.dll writes a minidump for every crash to Steam's `dumps` folder, for example `C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\dumps\crash_cof.exe_<time>_1.dmp`. Open it with `cdb -z <file> -c ".ecxr; kb 20; lm o; q"` (the Windows debugger) and name the frames with `python tools/name_addr.py <dll> <offset> ...`, which reads the baseline link maps. The `lm o` list shows what else was loaded: runtime tools such as RebuiltSimon or the co-op mod crash in their own modules, and those crashes do not belong here.
